# Naruto pack: canon jutsu research

Research for turning the canon jutsu of the `naruto` fan pack's characters into game techniques. It
covers every character in `src/content/packs/naruto/people/*.ts` and every canon-named enemy (Gōzu,
Meizu, Zabuza, Haku, Dosu, Kin, Raiga), and checks what the game already has against the wiki.

## Sources and conventions

- **Source:** Narutopedia (<https://naruto.fandom.com>), read through its MediaWiki API
  (`api.php?action=parse&prop=links` for each character page, then `action=query&prop=revisions`
  for the linked pages' wikitext, following redirects). Fetched on 2026-10-14. Nothing here is
  from memory: every row comes from a page's `{{Infobox/Jutsu}}` and first paragraph, and links
  to it.
- **Who counts as a user:** a character is listed under a jutsu only if they appear in that page's
  `users=` field. Pages without a jutsu infobox (tools such as Explosive Tag, concepts such as
  Chakra Flow) are not jutsu and are skipped. A jutsu page that no character page links to would
  be missed; I also scanned every fetched jutsu page's `users=` field for the characters, which
  found a few more, but pages linked from nowhere were not fetched.
- **Basic Academy techniques are invisible here.** Clone Technique and Body Replacement
  Technique have no `users=` field at all, Transformation Technique and Body Flicker Technique
  list only a handful of people (Body Flicker's field carries an editor's note that only those
  who fight with it are listed). So nobody in the pack shows them, though the game rightly gives
  them out.
- **Media markers:** in `users=` a bare name is canon in the page's own primary medium (manga if
  the jutsu has a manga debut, otherwise anime, otherwise the one medium it has); `~anime`,
  `~novel`, `~game`, `~movie` mark other media; `~with~X` marks a collaboration with X. Tables
  give the medium as manga, anime, novel, game or movie (several when the page lists several).
- **Era:** taken from the jutsu's debut in the medium the character uses it in. Manga: chapter
  ≤ 244 is Part I, later chapters Shippuden, a Boruto chapter (`boruto=Yes`) or a spin-off manga
  (Sasuke Retsuden, Konoha Shinden, Two Blue Vortex) is Boruto. Anime: `debut shippuden=Yes` is
  Shippuden, `boruto anime=Yes` is Boruto, otherwise Part I. **This is the technique's debut, not
  the character's first use**: Rasengan (chapter 150) counts as Part I for Kakashi too, and
  Amaterasu (chapter 148, Itachi) as Part I for Sasuke. The game is set in the Part I genin era,
  so check the character's own arc before giving a Part I technique to a genin.
- **Fields:** "not stated" means the infobox leaves the field out (many Part I pages have no rank).
  Nature is the infobox's `jutsu type` (it includes Yin and Yang Release). "Needs" collects
  kekkei genkai and clan (hiden) requirements, weapon classifications (Bukijutsu, Kenjutsu,
  Shurikenjutsu) and hand signs (listed when short, counted when long).
- **Descriptions** are one line in my own words from the page's first paragraph.
- **"In the game"** gives the game's `TechniqueDef` id for the same jutsu (matched by name,
  romaji or the dub name, checked against the game's description) and any difference in rank,
  element, discipline or reach. A game technique with no `reach` uses its art's usual reach and
  is not compared.

## Discrepancies with the game

Everything below is a place where the game's techniques (`src/content/packs/naruto/**/*.ts`) or
who it gives them to (`people/arsenals.ts`, teachers' `teaches`, enemy kits) disagree with the
wiki. Some are deliberate game design (teachers passing on techniques they never used, a
learnable kit for every art); they are listed so the choice is a conscious one.

### The ones asked about

1. **The Wind Blade school is game-invented.** `trench-knife-rush` (D) and `wind-blade-flurry`
   (B), both `school: 'Wind Blade'`, have no Narutopedia page: searching the wiki for "Trench
   Knife Rush", "Wind Blade Flurry" and "Wind Blade" finds nothing of those names. Asuma's trench
   knives are the [Chakra Blade](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Chakra_Blade) tool page ("uniquely
   designed trench knives worn like brass knuckles"). His canon blade jutsu are
   [Flying Swallow](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Flying_Swallow) (manga chapter 141, Part I,
   B-rank; the game has it as `flying-swallow`, C-rank and wind),
   [Chakra Blade: Straight Line](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Chakra_Blade:_Straight_Line)
   (manga chapter 325, Shippuden) and
   [Wind Release: Verdant Mountain Gale](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Wind_Release:_Verdant_Mountain_Gale)
   (anime, Shippuden). Flying Swallow's infobox gives no nature; the wind link comes from the
   [Chakra Flow](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Chakra_Flow) page, where Asuma explains flowing
   wind chakra through blades. `chakra-flow` itself has no jutsu page (Chakra Flow is a concept
   page), and `seven-swords-dance`, which Asuma teaches, has no page either.
   **Fixed in #82:** Asuma fights with Flying Swallow (now B-rank, close reach, no
   school), the new `chakra-blade-straight-line` and `verdant-mountain-gale` (both B, his to teach)
   and Burning Ash. Wind Blade stays in the game as a non-canon school whose drills he teaches, but
   it is off his arsenal.
2. **Asuma never uses Shadow Clone.** The
   [Shadow Clone Technique](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Shadow_Clone_Technique) `users=` field
   lists Kakashi, Naruto, Sasuke, Kiba, Hiruzen and Hayate (manga), Ebisu and Yūgao (anime) among
   the pack's characters, but not Asuma in any medium. `ASUMA_ARSENAL` gives it to him (its
   comment says "the clones he fought the Akatsuki with"). **Fixed in #82:** off his arsenal.
3. **Kakashi's Lightning Beast Running and Lightning Hound are one jutsu.** Narutopedia has no
   page or redirect titled "Lightning Beast Running" today (the API reports it missing); a wiki
   search for it returns
   [Lightning Release: Lightning Beast Tracking Fang](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Lightning_Release:_Lightning_Beast_Tracking_Fang)
   first, whose infobox gives `game names=Lightning Beast Running Technique` and `other
names=Lightning Hound`. It is C-rank, Lightning Release, short to mid range, manga chapter 421
   (Shippuden). The game has it twice, `lightning-beast-running` and `lightning-hound`, both
   B-rank, and `KAKASHI_ARSENAL` lists both. One of them should go (or become a variant), and the
   rank is C. **Fixed in #82:** `lightning-beast-running` is merged into `lightning-hound`
   (renamed Lightning Style: Lightning Beast Tracking Fang, C-rank); a save migration moves anyone
   who knew it over.
4. **Kakashi's Water Wall matches.**
   [Water Release: Water Formation Wall](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Water_Release:_Water_Formation_Wall)
   (dub "Water Style: Water Wall") is manga, B-rank, Water Release, chapter 120 (Part I), with
   Kakashi a manga user. The game's `water-wall` (B, water, ninjutsu) agrees.
5. **One Thousand Years of Death is Kakashi's, not Guy's.**
   [One Thousand Years of Death](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/One_Thousand_Years_of_Death) lists
   Kakashi and Naruto as manga users (Obito and Sasuke only in games); Guy is not listed. It is
   E-rank and, per the page, not actually harmful. The game files `thousand-years-of-death` (D)
   under Guy's `school: 'Strong Fist'` and has Guy teach it; it fits Kakashi's arsenal (where it
   also is) better than the Strong Fist school. **Fixed in #82:** E-rank, no school, taught by Kakashi
   (not Guy), still in Kakashi's arsenal.

### Other contradictions worth fixing

- **Two game techniques for one jutsu.** `dragon-fire` (D) and `fire-dragon-flame` (B, named
  after the dub "Fire Style: Dragon Flame Jutsu") are both
  [Fire Release: Dragon Fire Technique](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Fire_Release:_Dragon_Fire_Technique)
  (C-rank; Sasuke runs it along wire, which is what both game descriptions describe).
  `uzumaki-chains` (E) and `adamantine-chains` (C) are both
  [Adamantine Sealing Chains](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Adamantine_Sealing_Chains) (A-rank,
  manga chapter 498, Shippuden; only Kushina is a canon user, Karin in games). **Fixed in
  #82:** `fire-dragon-flame` is merged into `dragon-fire` (Fire Style: Dragon Fire
  Technique, C-rank, on the Archives shelves; a save migration moves anyone who knew it over).
  `adamantine-chains` is A-rank; `uzumaki-chains`, the Uzumaki starting technique (which must be
  rank E), is now the Uzumaki Sealing Thread, a game-invented first lesson rather than the chains.
- **`leaf-whirlwind` mixes two kicks.** Its name and romaji ("Konoha Senpū") are
  [Leaf Whirlwind](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Leaf_Whirlwind) (a high kick that sets up a low
  kick, D-rank), but its description, "a low spinning kick that sweeps the legs", is
  [Leaf Gale](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Leaf_Gale) (Konoha Reppū, D-rank). Both are manga
  chapter 37, both Lee and Guy. The game's E rank matches neither. **Fixed in #82:** it is the
  high kick at D-rank; Leaf Gale is item 6 of the checklist.
- **Raiga fights with other people's jutsu.** His canon list is all lightning (Lightning Ball,
  Fangs of Lightning, Lightning Burial: Banquet of Lightning, Lightning Dragon Tornado, Lightning
  Strike Armour, Rock Avalanche, plus Hiding in Mist in the anime). The game gives him
  `chidori-sword` ([Sword of Kusanagi: Chidori Katana](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Sword_of_Kusanagi:_Chidori_Katana),
  Sasuke's, Shippuden), `false-darkness`
  ([Lightning Release: False Darkness](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Lightning_Release:_False_Darkness),
  Kakuzu's, Shippuden, B-rank) and the invented `decapitating-sweep`. The wiki's Raiga entries are
  all anime (anime-only pages, or marked `~anime`). **Fixed in #87:** Raiga's kit is now his canon lightning (Fangs of
  Lightning, Banquet of Lightning, Lightning Dragon Tornado, Lightning Strike Armour, Rock
  Avalanche).
- **Teachers' lists name techniques of people outside the pack** (each page linked in the full
  list below): Kurenai teaches Itachi's Sharingan illusion Mirror Heaven and Earth Change; Anko
  teaches the Eight Trigrams Sealing Style and Four Symbols Seal (Minato, Kushina, Obito); Raidō
  teaches Flying Thunder God Slash (Minato, Tobirama); Aoba teaches Bringer-of-Darkness
  (Hashirama; Tobirama in the anime); Ibiki teaches Shackling Stakes (Itachi, Sasuke; needs the
  Sharingan); Yūgao teaches Sasuke's Chidori Katana; Hana teaches Sakura's Cherry Blossom Impact;
  Naruto teaches Kushina's Adamantine Sealing Chains.
- **Enemy kits borrowed from others:** Dosu casts Hell Viewing (Kakashi's; his own is Resonating
  Echo Drill); Kin plays Phantom Sound Chains (Tayuya's and Kabuto's; hers are Bell Sound Clone
  and Shadow Senbon) and False Surroundings (Kotetsu's); Haku is not listed for Body Flicker or
  Mystical Palm; Gōzu is not listed for Hiding in Mist and Meizu not for Water Bullet (the
  brothers' one listed jutsu is
  [Water Release: Hiding in Water Technique](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Water_Release:_Hiding_in_Water_Technique)). **Fixed in #85 and #87:** Dosu uses the Resonating Echo Drill, Kin
  Shadow Senbon and the Bell Sound Clone, Haku the Thousand Flying Water Needles and Silent
  Killing, and the Demon Brothers Hiding in Water; Zabuza fights with Water Clone and Flying
  Revolving Sword (#83) in place of the invented Decapitating Sweep.
- **Discipline differences.** The wiki classes Clone Technique, Transformation Technique, Mind
  Body Switch, Mind Body Disturbance and Scattering Thousand Crows as ninjutsu (the game:
  genjutsu); Partial Multi-Size as ninjutsu (game: taijutsu); Flying Thunder God Slash as
  space–time ninjutsu and bukijutsu (game: kenjutsu); Twin Rising Dragons as ninjutsu,
  fūinjutsu and shurikenjutsu (game: kenjutsu); Four Violet Flames as a barrier ninjutsu (game:
  fūinjutsu). Some of these are reasonable fits to the game's five arts; they are listed for
  completeness.
- **Element differences.** `summoning-ninken` has no element, but its page,
  [Summoning: Earth Release: Tracking Fang Technique](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Summoning:_Earth_Release:_Tracking_Fang_Technique),
  is Earth Release (B-rank). `flying-swallow` is wind in the game, no nature on the wiki (see 1).
- **Shippuden techniques in a Part I game.** These game techniques debut after chapter 244 (or
  in Shippuden episodes): `leaf-rising-wind`, `cherry-blossom-impact`, `morning-peacock`,
  `daytime-tiger`, `chakra-sensing`, `water-bullet`, `false-darkness`, `great-fire-annihilation`,
  `shackling-stakes`, `toad-confrontation-chant`, `samurai-sabre`, `chidori-sword`,
  `flying-thunder-god-slash`, `ash-pile-burning`, `vacuum-sphere`, `four-pillar-bind`,
  `lightning-beast-running`, `lightning-hound`, `lightning-clone`, `uzumaki-chains`,
  `adamantine-chains`, `air-palm`, `crow-scatter`, `syrup-capture-field`, `gale-palm`.
- **Thinly attested matches.** `water-bullet` ("spit a pressurised ball of water") is matched to
  [Water Release: Water Bullet Technique](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Water_Release:_Water_Bullet_Technique)
  (a torrent, rank not stated, chapter 612), the nearest page by name. `four-pillar-bind` is
  lightning in the game and the page found is
  [Lightning Release: Four Pillar Bind](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Lightning_Release:_Four_Pillar_Bind)
  (its only listed user is Zō; Shippuden). `chakra-sensing` is matched to the general
  [Sensing Technique](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Sensing_Technique).

### Game techniques with no Narutopedia jutsu page

No jutsu page exists under these names (or the page found has no jutsu infobox). Most are the game's own generic kit (strikes, tags, basic elemental moves); the named ones (the Wind Blade school, `moonlight-stance`, `iai-kamiari`, `seven-swords-dance`, `petal-storm`, `snake-binding-seal`, `interrogators-gaze`, `black-blade-draw`, `decapitating-sweep`, `shadow-clone-sword-dance`, `tree-binding`) read as canon but are invented.

`shuriken-jutsu`, `tree-binding`, `deflecting-guard`, `chakra-flow`, `wheel-cut`, `swallow-return`, `hilt-and-edge`, `decapitating-sweep`, `shadow-clone-sword-dance`, `iai-kamiari`, `seven-swords-dance`, `explosive-tag` ([Explosive Tag](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Explosive_Tag) is a tool or concept page, not a jutsu), `chakra-suppression-seal` ([Chakra-Suppressing Seal](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Chakra-Suppressing_Seal) is a tool or concept page, not a jutsu), `explosive-tag-trap`, `smoke-tag` ([Smoke Tag](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Smoke_Tag) is a tool or concept page, not a jutsu), `paralysis-tag`, `chain-detonation`, `chakra-absorbing-seal`, `mass-tag-detonation`, `basic-strike-combination`, `leg-sweep`, `shoulder-throw`, `rising-knee`, `arm-bar`, `flying-kick`, `heel-drop`, `binding-illusion`, `sapping-illusion`, `illusory-body`, `bleeding-slash`, `binding-tag`, `chakra-leech-tag`, `binding-wire`, `air-bullet`, `water-gun`, `mud-bullet`, `static-palm`, `vacuum-bullet`, `moonlight-stance`, `trench-knife-rush`, `wind-blade-flurry`, `petal-storm`, `snake-binding-seal`, `interrogators-gaze`, `senbon-needles`, `black-blade-draw`.

### Who the game gives techniques to

Arsenal, `teaches` and enemy-kit entries whose wiki page exists, names users, and does not list the character. (Each character section below also lists every assignment, matching or not.)

- Kakashi Hatake is not a listed user of: `body-flicker` (arsenal) on [Body Flicker Technique](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Body_Flicker_Technique) (no in-game character is listed); `water-prison` (teaches) on [Water Prison Technique](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Water_Prison_Technique) (in-game characters the wiki does list: Zabuza Momochi).

## Not yet in the game

Every manga or anime jutsu of an in-game character, Part I or Shippuden, that has no game equivalent: 275 items in 19 batches of 15. Each jutsu appears once with every in-game user (anime-only users marked). Order: Part I manga, Part I anime, Shippuden manga, Shippuden anime; within each, by how central the character is (senseis, then Team 7, Team 8, Team 10 and Guy's team, then instructors, clan heads, jōnin, chūnin and enemies). A jutsu used in the manga by anyone counts as manga.

Left out: Boruto-era jutsu, novel-, game- and movie-only jutsu, and these pages that aren't techniques to add:

- [Summoning Technique](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Summoning_Technique): umbrella for every summon; the game models summons as specific techniques (summoning-ninken).
- [Strong Fist](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Strong_Fist): a fighting style; it exists in the game as Guy's school name (`school: 'Strong Fist'`).
- [Genjutsu: Sharingan](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Genjutsu:_Sharingan): umbrella term for illusions cast through the Sharingan.
- [Six Paths Technique](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Six_Paths_Technique): umbrella for the seven Rinnegan paths, each listed separately.
- [Formation Ino–Shika–Chō](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Formation_Ino%E2%80%93Shika%E2%80%93Ch%C5%8D): umbrella for the trio's formations; Human Bullet Yo-Yo is listed separately.
- [Inner Sakura](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Inner_Sakura): a personality, not a combat technique.
- [Naka Shrine Pass Technique](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Naka_Shrine_Pass_Technique): a password-like seal sequence, not a combat technique.
- [Torii Seal](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Torii_Seal): the appearance of Naruto's seal, not a technique.
- [Chidori](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Chidori): the same technique as Lightning Cutter, which the game has as `lightning-blade` (the wiki calls Lightning Cutter "the special name for Kakashi's Chidori"); a Sasuke version would be a rename, not a new technique.

Remember the era caveat above: a Part I jutsu may be one the character only uses later.

Progress: `[x]` is added to the game (with its id and the PR), `[-]` is skipped with the reason. A skipped item doesn't count towards a batch of 15, so the game's batches run a few items past the headings below. All 275 items are now settled: 160 added as techniques, 115 skipped.

### Batch 1 (items 1–15)

_Part I, manga_

1. [x] [Area Scanning Technique](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Area_Scanning_Technique): Chakra sent through a surface by touch maps the area and finds who is in it. Rank not stated, no nature, Ninjutsu. In-game users: Kakashi Hatake, Sasuke Uchiha, Sakura Haruno. Added in #83 as `area-scanning`.
2. [x] [Earth Release: Hiding Like a Mole Technique](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Earth_Release:_Hiding_Like_a_Mole_Technique): Softens earth so the user can tunnel through it like a mole. Rank C, earth release, Ninjutsu. In-game users: Kakashi Hatake. Added in #83 as `hiding-like-a-mole`.
3. [x] [Flying Revolving Sword](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Flying_Revolving_Sword): The Kubikiribōchō thrown so it spins into the foe. Rank not stated, no nature, Kenjutsu, Shurikenjutsu. In-game users: Kakashi Hatake (anime), Zabuza Momochi. Added in #83 as `flying-revolving-sword`.
4. [x] [Water Clone Technique](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Water_Clone_Technique): Solid clones made from water that can fight but are weaker than the user. Rank C, water release, Ninjutsu, Clone Techniques. In-game users: Kakashi Hatake, Zabuza Momochi. Added in #83 as `water-clone`.
5. [x] [Water Release: Water Shark Bullet Technique](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Water_Release:_Water_Shark_Bullet_Technique): Water hurled in the shape of a shark. Rank B, water release, Ninjutsu. In-game users: Kakashi Hatake. Added in #83 as `water-shark-bullet`.
6. [x] [Leaf Gale](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Leaf_Gale): A spinning low kick from behind that knocks the target off balance. Rank D, no nature, Taijutsu. In-game users: Might Guy, Rock Lee. Added in #83 as `leaf-gale`.
7. [-] [Cursed Seal of Heaven](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Cursed_Seal_of_Heaven): One of Orochimaru's two strongest cursed seals, granting power at a cost. Rank not stated, no nature, Juinjutsu, Kinjutsu, Senjutsu. In-game users: Anko Mitarashi, Sasuke Uchiha. Skipped in #83: a cursed seal is a state Orochimaru puts on someone, not a technique anyone learns or casts.
8. [x] [Hidden Shadow Snake Hands](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Hidden_Shadow_Snake_Hands): Snakes shoot from the sleeve to bite, bind or anchor. Rank C, no nature, Ninjutsu, Space–Time Ninjutsu. In-game users: Anko Mitarashi, Sasuke Uchiha. Added in #83 as `hidden-shadow-snake-hands`.
9. [x] [Twin Snakes Mutual Death Technique](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Twin_Snakes_Mutual_Death_Technique): The user forces the victim's hand into the seals and both die. Rank A, no nature, Ninjutsu, Kinjutsu. In-game users: Anko Mitarashi. Added in #83 as `twin-snakes-mutual-death`.
10. [x] [All Directions Shuriken](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/All_Directions_Shuriken): Naruto and a crowd of shadow clones throw shuriken from every side at once, leaving nowhere to dodge. Rank A, no nature, Ninjutsu, Shurikenjutsu, Clone Techniques, Kinjutsu. In-game users: Naruto Uzumaki. Added in #83 as `all-directions-shuriken`.
11. [x] [Clone Body Blow](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Clone_Body_Blow): Naruto and a few shadow clones improvise a combined attack to land a blow from afar. Rank A, no nature, Taijutsu, Ninjutsu, Clone Techniques. In-game users: Naruto Uzumaki. Added in #83 as `clone-body-blow`.
12. [-] [Combination Transformation](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Combination_Transformation): Two or more users transform together into one form and share its strength. Rank B, no nature, Ninjutsu, Cooperation Ninjutsu. In-game users: Naruto Uzumaki, Sasuke Uchiha (anime), Kiba Inuzuka. Skipped in #83: two users merge into one form; there is no combat shape for it (a combination technique needs two halves that each act).
13. [-] [Great Roar](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Great_Roar): A tailed beast's chakra-laden roar that blasts like a shockwave. Rank not stated, no nature, Ninjutsu. In-game users: Naruto Uzumaki. Skipped in #83: a tailed beast’s own power, not a technique a person learns.
14. [x] [Harem Technique](https://naruto.fandom.com/wiki/Harem_Technique): Shadow clones all use Sexy Technique to swarm and stun the target. Rank A, no nature, Ninjutsu, Clone Techniques. In-game users: Naruto Uzumaki. Added in #83 as `harem-technique`.
